Tool reply2026-09-12T23:18:50.385Z
1	"""Scenario catalogue: the bundled JSON must load, and its private half must stay private."""
2	
3	from __future__ import annotations
4	
5	import pytest
6	
7	from faultline_common.schemas import Scenario
8	from sandbox_env import grader, scenarios
9	
10	#: the scenarios this service must always ship. Extra scenarios may be added to the catalogue by
11	#: other workstreams (PLAN.md 2.11 adds `worker-crash`), so every test below asserts "at least the
12	#: core four, and every scenario actually on disk is valid" instead of pinning an exact set — a
13	#: catalogue test that breaks when a scenario is *added* tests the directory listing, not the code.
14	CORE = {"missing-config", "locked-file", "lost-ack", "gauntlet"}
15	ALL_IDS = sorted(b.id for b in scenarios.list_bundles())
16	
17	#: public fields every scenario must expose; `Scenario` may grow more (they are public by design).
18	PUBLIC_REQUIRED = {"id", "title", "description", "task_prompt", "max_steps", "fault_kinds"}
19	#: private halves that must never appear in the public projection
20	PUBLIC_FORBIDDEN = {"fault_plan", "hidden_tests", "setup", "fixture"}
21	
22	
23	def test_all_bundled_scenarios_load() -> None:
24	    ids = set(ALL_IDS)
25	    assert CORE <= ids, f"missing bundled scenario(s): {sorted(CORE - ids)}"
26	
27	
28	@pytest.mark.parametrize("scenario_id", ALL_IDS)
29	def test_public_view_is_a_valid_scenario_and_leaks_no_plan(scenario_id: str) -> None:
30	    """The public view may name files (the task is *about* files); it must not reveal the plan.
31	
32	    What has to stay hidden is the mechanism — which path fails, in which mode, for how many hits,
33	    and with what delay. `fault_kinds` is deliberately published (the UI shows fault badges, and
34	    the harness prompt tells the agent that tools can fail) but it is kind-only: it never says
35	    where or when. A sticky `missing_file` is a special case — the file is genuinely gone, so the
36	    agent will discover the path the moment it looks, and nothing is gained by coyness in prose.
37	    """
38	    bundle = scenarios.get_bundle(scenario_id)
39	    public = bundle.public
40	    assert isinstance(public, Scenario)
41	    assert public.task_prompt.strip()
42	    assert public.max_steps >= 10
43	
44	    dumped = public.model_dump()
45	    assert PUBLIC_REQUIRED <= set(dumped)
46	    assert not (PUBLIC_FORBIDDEN & set(dumped))
47	    assert set(public.fault_kinds) == {f.kind for f in bundle.fault_plan.faults}
48	
49	    blob = public.model_dump_json()
50	    for word in ("sticky", "transient", "delay_ms", "fault_plan", "hidden_tests", '"hits"'):
51	        assert word not in blob, f"{scenario_id} leaks fault mechanics via {word!r}"
52	
53	    # `description` is reviewer-facing UI copy and is allowed to explain the scenario ("the first
54	    # read hits a flaky filesystem"). `task_prompt` is the only field that reaches the model, so
55	    # that is where a transient target would actually give the game away: naming it would tell the
56	    # agent that an ENOENT on a file which is really on disk must be fake.
57	    prompt = public.task_prompt
58	    for fault in bundle.fault_plan.faults:
59	        if fault.mode == "transient" and fault.kind == "missing_file":
60	            assert fault.path not in prompt, f"{scenario_id} leaks the transient target {fault.path}"
61	    for word in ("EACCES", "ENOENT", "injected", "fault plan"):
62	        assert word not in prompt, f"{scenario_id} task_prompt leaks mechanics via {word!r}"
63	
64

66	def test_every_check_id_is_implemented(scenario_id: str) -> None:
67	    bundle = scenarios.get_bundle(scenario_id)
68	    assert bundle.checks, f"{scenario_id} has no checks"
69	    for spec in bundle.checks:
70	        assert spec.id in grader.CHECKS, f"{scenario_id} references unknown check {spec.id}"
71	        assert spec.weight > 0

38	def test_reset_observe_delete_lifecycle(api: httpx.Client, fake_ws) -> None:
39	    r = api.post("/episodes", json={"scenario_id": "missing-config", "seed": 3})
40	    assert r.status_code == 200
41	    reset = ResetResponse.model_validate(r.json())
42	    assert reset.scenario.id == "missing-config"
43	    assert CONFIG not in {f.path for f in reset.files}, "the sticky fault is applied before reset returns"
44	
45	    obs = ObserveResponse.model_validate(api.get(f"/episodes/{reset.episode_id}").json())
46	    assert obs.step == 0 and obs.done is False and obs.faults_fired == []
47	
48	    # the agent recreates the config
49	    fake_ws.files[CONFIG] = '{"capacity": 10, "refill_per_sec": 2.0, "burst_multiplier": 1.5}'
50	    obs = ObserveResponse.model_validate(api.get(f"/episodes/{reset.episode_id}").json())
51	    assert next(f for f in obs.files if f.path == CONFIG).status == "added"
52	
53	    d = api.delete(f"/episodes/{reset.episode_id}")
54	    assert d.status_code == 200 and d.json()["terminated"] is True
55	    assert fake_ws.terminated is True

58	def test_evaluate_scores_a_good_run(api: httpx.Client, fake_ws) -> None:
59	    reset = ResetResponse.model_validate(
60	        api.post("/episodes", json={"scenario_id": "missing-config"}).json()
61	    )
62	    fake_ws.responses["pytest"] = ExecResult(stdout=GREEN, exit_code=0, duration_ms=90)
63	    fake_ws.files[CONFIG] = '{"capacity": 10, "refill_per_sec": 2.0, "burst_multiplier": 1.5}'
64	
65	    # a ledger that shows the recovery we want to reward
66	    ep = episodes.load(reset.episode_id)
67	    ep["ledger"] = [
68	        {"step": 1, "ts": "2026-09-12T00:00:00.000Z", "tool": "read_file", "args_digest": "a1",
69	         "path": "README.md", "mutating": False, "outcome": "short_circuit", "duration_ms": 2,
70	         "fault": {"step": 1, "kind": "missing_file", "path": "README.md", "mode": "transient"}},
71	        {"step": 2, "ts": "2026-09-12T00:00:01.000Z", "tool": "read_file", "args_digest": "a1",
72	         "path": "README.md", "mutating": False, "outcome": "ok", "duration_ms": 2},
73	        {"step": 3, "ts": "2026-09-12T00:00:02.000Z", "tool": "write_file", "args_digest": "a2",
74	         "path": CONFIG, "mutating": True, "outcome": "ok", "duration_ms": 3},
75	    ]
76	    episodes.save(ep)
77	
78	    res = EvaluateResponse.model_validate(
79	        api.post(f"/episodes/{reset.episode_id}/evaluate").json()
80	    )
81	    assert res.passed is True
82	    assert res.tests.passed == 8 and res.tests.failed == 0
83	    assert {c.id: c.ok for c in res.checks} == {
84	        "config_valid": True, "retried_transient_read": True, "no_thrash": True
85	    }
86	    assert res.score == 100.0
87	    assert len(res.ledger) == 3, "the full ledger is released only at evaluate time"
88	
89	    # the hidden tests were uploaded, run and removed again
90	    assert any(".faultline_eval" in c for c in fake_ws.commands)
91	    assert not any(p.startswith(".faultline_eval") for p in fake_ws.files)
92

181	async def test_transient_missing_file_then_recovery(live_server, fake_ws):
182	    """The scenario the `missing-config` grader checks: ENOENT once, real content next time."""
183	    ep = episodes.reset("missing-config")
184	    async with client(live_server, ep.episode_id) as c:
185	        first = await c.call_tool("read_file", {"path": README}, raise_on_error=False)
186	        second = await c.call_tool("read_file", {"path": README}, raise_on_error=False)
187	
188	    assert first.is_error
189	    body = payload(first)
190	    assert body["code"] == "ENOENT"
191	    assert body["error"] == "read_file: README.md: No such file or directory"
192	
193	    assert not second.is_error
194	    assert "ratelimiter" in payload(second)["content"]
195	
196	    ledger = episodes.load(ep.episode_id)["ledger"]
197	    assert [e["outcome"] for e in ledger] == ["short_circuit", "ok"]
198	    # subset, not equality: `FaultFired` gains provenance fields (origin/layer/description) as
199	    # PLAN.md 2.11 lands, and this test is about *which* fault fired, not the model's field list.
200	    fired = ledger[0]["fault"]
201	    assert {k: fired[k] for k in ("step", "kind", "path", "mode")} == {
202	        "step": 1, "kind": "missing_file", "path": README, "mode": "transient"
203	    }
204	    assert ledger[1]["fault"] is None

207	async def test_missing_file_via_run_command_is_a_shell_style_failure(live_server, fake_ws):
208	    ep = episodes.reset("missing-config")
209	    async with client(live_server, ep.episode_id) as c:
210	        res = await c.call_tool("run_command", {"command": f"cat {README}"}, raise_on_error=False)
211	    assert not res.is_error, "a shell does not raise; it exits non-zero"
212	    body = payload(res)
213	    assert body["exit_code"] == 1
214	    assert body["stderr"].strip() == "cat: README.md: No such file or directory"
215	    assert body["stdout"] == ""
216	    assert episodes.load(ep.episode_id)["ledger"][-1]["outcome"] == "short_circuit"
217

219	async def test_listing_hides_the_vanished_file_without_burning_a_hit(live_server, fake_ws):
220	    """The illusion has to be self-consistent: if read says ENOENT, ls must not show the file."""
221	    ep = episodes.reset("missing-config")
222	    async with client(live_server, ep.episode_id) as c:
223	        before = payload(await c.call_tool("list_dir", {"path": "."}))
224	        assert README not in [e["name"] for e in before["entries"]]
225	        # the listing did not consume the single hit, so the read still fails
226	        failed = await c.call_tool("read_file", {"path": README}, raise_on_error=False)
227	        assert failed.is_error
228	        after = payload(await c.call_tool("list_dir", {"path": "."}))
229	    assert README in [e["name"] for e in after["entries"]]
230	    # config/settings.json is sticky: genuinely deleted, so it is absent in both listings
231	    assert "config" not in [e["name"] for e in after["entries"]] or True
232

234	async def test_sticky_missing_file_is_really_gone(live_server, fake_ws):
235	    ep = episodes.reset("missing-config")
236	    async with client(live_server, ep.episode_id) as c:
237	        res = await c.call_tool("read_file", {"path": "config/settings.json"}, raise_on_error=False)
238	    assert res.is_error and payload(res)["code"] == "ENOENT"
239	    # a REAL ENOENT, not an injected one: no fault recorded against it
240	    assert episodes.load(ep.episode_id)["ledger"][-1]["fault"] is None

246	async def test_denied_write_blocks_two_attempts_then_lifts(live_server, fake_ws):
247	    ep = episodes.reset("locked-file")
248	    original = fake_ws.files[LIMITS]
249	    fixed = original.replace("burst_multiplier) - 1", "burst_multiplier)")
250	
251	    async with client(live_server, ep.episode_id) as c:
252	        for _ in range(2):
253	            res = await c.call_tool("write_file", {"path": LIMITS, "content": fixed}, raise_on_error=False)
254	            assert res.is_error
255	            body = payload(res)
256	            assert body["code"] == "EACCES"
257	            assert body["error"] == "write_file: src/ratelimiter/limits.py: Permission denied"
258	            assert fake_ws.files[LIMITS] == original, "a denied write must not land"
259	        ok = await c.call_tool("write_file", {"path": LIMITS, "content": fixed})
260	
261	    assert not ok.is_error
262	    assert fake_ws.files[LIMITS] == fixed
263	    outcomes = [e["outcome"] for e in episodes.load(ep.episode_id)["ledger"]]
264	    assert outcomes == ["short_circuit", "short_circuit", "ok"]
265

267	async def test_denied_write_also_catches_shell_writes(live_server, fake_ws):
268	    ep = episodes.reset("locked-file")
269	    async with client(live_server, ep.episode_id) as c:
270	        res = await c.call_tool(
271	            "run_command", {"command": f"sed -i 's/- 1//' {LIMITS}"}, raise_on_error=False
272	        )
273	    body = payload(res)
274	    assert body["exit_code"] == 1
275	    assert body["stderr"].strip() == "bash: src/ratelimiter/limits.py: Permission denied"
276	    assert f"sed -i 's/- 1//' {LIMITS}" not in fake_ws.commands, "the command must not have run"

def test_score_formula() -> None:
    green = TestsResult(passed=6)
    red = TestsResult(passed=1, failed=2)
    all_ok = [Check(id="a", ok=True, weight=2.0), Check(id="b", ok=True, weight=1.0)]
    half = [Check(id="a", ok=True, weight=2.0), Check(id="b", ok=False, weight=2.0)]
    none_ok = [Check(id="a", ok=False, weight=1.0)]

    assert grader.score_for(green, all_ok) == 100.0
    assert grader.score_for(green, half) == 80.0
    assert grader.score_for(green, none_ok) == 60.0
    assert grader.score_for(red, all_ok) == 40.0
    assert grader.score_for(red, none_ok) == 0.0
    # weights matter: a 2.0-weight check is worth twice a 1.0 one
    assert grader.score_for(red, [Check(id="a", ok=True, weight=2.0),
                                  Check(id="b", ok=False, weight=1.0)]) == pytest.approx(26.7)


# --------------------------------------------------------------------------- config_valid

    assert entry["outcome"] == "ack_lost"
    assert entry["fault"]["kind"] == "ack_lost"


async def test_ack_lost_fires_once(live_server, fake_ws):
    ep = episodes.reset("lost-ack")
    zero_delay(ep.episode_id)
    async with client(live_server, ep.episode_id) as c:
        first = await c.call_tool("write_file", {"path": CHANGELOG, "content": "a", "mode": "append"},
                                  raise_on_error=False)
        second = await c.call_tool("write_file", {"path": CHANGELOG, "content": "b", "mode": "append"},
                                   raise_on_error=False)
    assert first.is_error and not second.is_error


# --------------------------------------------------------------------------- ledger & steps


async def test_every_call_appends_a_ledger_entry_and_bumps_the_step(live_server, fake_ws):
    ep = episodes.reset("lost-ack")
    async with client(live_server, ep.episode_id) as c:
        await c.call_tool("list_dir", {"path": "."})
        await c.call_tool("read_file", {"path": README})
        await c.call_tool("run_command", {"command": "ls -la"})
        await c.call_tool("write_file", {"path": "x.txt", "content": "1"})
    state = episodes.load(ep.episode_id)
    assert state["step"] == 4
    assert [e["step"] for e in state["ledger"]] == [1, 2, 3, 4]
    assert [e["tool"] for e in state["ledger"]] == ["list_dir", "read_file", "run_command", "write_file"]
    assert [e["mutating"] for e in state["ledger"]] == [False, False, False, True]
    assert all(e["args_digest"] for e in state["ledger"])


async def test_calls_after_delete_are_rejected(live_server, fake_ws):
    ep = episodes.reset("lost-ack")
    episodes.delete(ep.episode_id)
    async with client(live_server, ep.episode_id) as c:
        res = await c.call_tool("list_dir", {"path": "."}, raise_on_error=False)
    assert res.is_error
    assert payload(res)["code"] == "EINVAL"


# --------------------------------------------------------------------------- transport details


def test_mcp_is_reachable_with_and_without_a_trailing_slash(live_server):
    """Both spellings hit the handler directly: a 307 here would double every tool call."""
    body = {"jsonrpc": "2.0", "id": 1, "method": "initialize",
            "params": {"protocolVersion": "2025-06-18", "capabilities": {},
                       "clientInfo": {"name": "t", "version": "1"}}}
    headers = {"Accept": "application/json, text/event-stream", "Content-Type": "application/json"}
    for path in ("/mcp", "/mcp/"):
        r = httpx.post(f"{live_server}{path}", json=body, headers=headers,
                       follow_redirects=False, timeout=20)
        assert r.status_code != 307, f"{path} redirected"
        assert r.status_code == 200, f"{path} -> {r.status_code} {r.text[:200]}"


def test_rest_routes_are_not_shadowed_by_the_mcp_mount(live_server):
    """The catch-all Mount('/') must come last: /health and /scenarios still answer."""
    assert httpx.get(f"{live_server}/health", timeout=10).status_code == 200
    assert httpx.get(f"{live_server}/scenarios", timeout=10).status_code == 200
    assert httpx.get(f"{live_server}/", timeout=10).json()["svc"] == "sandbox-env"

The main problem is doc drift. The provenance taxonomy (§2.11) is fully written up in schemas.py, FAULTS.md, GRADING.md, docs/error-taxonomy.md and ARCHITECTURE §5.4, but none of it is in the sandbox-env code yet. So the old outcome strings are still the live code path, not dead code. I also found two ways to kill live sandboxes and one grading-integrity gap. I edited nothing in the repo; the one-off checks I ran used an in-memory store and a scratchpad script.

1. Legacy / dead code (each "unused" grepped across the whole repo)

  • sandbox_env/util.py:27 sha256_hex: the only hit is its definition. Delete. High confidence, no risk.
  • workspace.py:533 local_tar_of (and its __all__ entry at :601): only the definition. Delete. High, none.
  • workspace.py:241-246, the helper's cptree op: nothing calls it, and it copies the snapshot op (:226). Delete. High, none.
  • Workspace.remove (workspace.py:99/395/517) and the helper's rm op (:232): the only .remove( hits are os.remove inside the helper. Delete. High, low.
  • scenarios.py:138 reload_catalogue says "tests only", but no test calls it. __init__.py:17 SVC has no users (the harness has its own config.SVC). Delete both. High, none.
  • grader.py:
  • line 25: unused Any import (ruff F401).
  • line 403: hasattr(episodes, "now_iso") is always true, because episodes.py:38 imports it.
  • Simplify both. High, none.
  • Stale docstrings:
  • episodes.py:69 mentions python -m sandbox_env dry runs, but there is no __main__.py.
  • workspace.py:425 says FakeWorkspace is used by scripts/; no script imports it.
  • Update both. High.
  • api.py:217 _episode_missing handler can't be reached, because every route catches EpisodeNotFound itself. Delete, or keep as a safety net. Low value.
  • tools/: neither script duplicates scripts/ or the verify skill (none of them touch sweep, reap or latency).
  • perf_probe.py is cited in docs/sandbox-env-ops.md:52.
  • lifecycle_proof.py is referenced nowhere; it produced runs/20260912T224357Z_lifecycle (17/17).
  • Keep both as evidence generators and link lifecycle_proof.py from the ops doc. Note it runs a real reap(keep_active=True) against the shared deployment (L160).
  • Duplicate data: graders/gauntlet/*.py are byte-identical to the three per-scenario graders, and overlays/gauntlet/limits.py is identical to overlays/locked-file/limits.py. Point gauntlet.json at the locked-file overlay; keep the grader copies, since hidden_tests takes a single directory. Medium, low. No unused fixtures.

2. Drift between code and docs

  • Taxonomy is described but not built. The docs are ahead of the code:
  • mcp_tools.py:150-173 never sets LedgerEntry.origin, error_code or interrupted.
  • A lost sandbox still returns EINTERNAL (mcp_tools.py:148,231,268,301,330); FAULTS.md:30 says ESANDBOX.
  • The /interruptions route is missing (api.py route map, lines 3-12).
  • Checked by running it: a sticky ENOENT gives the row {outcome:error, fault:None, origin:None} and faults_fired == [], but FAULTS.md:16 promises a staged entry.
  • scenarios.py:97-104 never fills checks, faults_public or harness_faults.
  • The unchecked box at PLAN:446 is accurate. Until the code lands, mark FAULTS.md, GRADING.md:37,57-65 and ARCH §5.4:411 as planned. High.
  • worker-crash.json is half-landed.
  • Its harness_faults never reach the public scenario (checked: []), and the harness has no code that reads them.
  • Its plan has no ack_lost fault, so verified_before_rewrite always reports "fault never triggered". A perfect run scores at most 84 (checked).
  • Take it out of the catalogue or finish §2.11. High.
  • read_file truncation doesn't match the description.
  • The tool description (mcp_tools.py:382) and PLAN:123 say long content is cut in the middle.
  • The helper already cuts at the cap (workspace.py:161; the fake at :460), so truncate_content (mcp_tools.py:280) never does anything.
  • Checked: a 40,000-char file comes back as 32,000 chars with no elision marker, and the last 8,000 chars are lost.
  • Fix the code. High, low risk.
  • PLAN §2.2 is out of date; the code and FAULTS.md are right:
  • run_command can also get ack_lost (faults.py:242,265).
  • list_dir never returns ENOENT; missing names are just hidden (faults.py:244).
  • The caps are characters, not KB (schemas.py:140).
  • Checks have per-check weights, not equal weights (grader.py:372); GRADING.md is right.
  • The ops routes GET /episodes and POST /episodes/sweep aren't listed, and ARCH §5.2 still says "Unchanged".
  • PLAN §2.6 / §3.1 details:
  • "≤3 write attempts" vs ≤4 in grader.py:196.
  • Path src/limits.py vs src/ratelimiter/limits.py.
  • No worker-crash row, and missing-config's no_thrash check isn't listed.
  • The plan is called "seeded", but the seed is stored (episodes.py:200) and never used.
  • The decide signature is (plan, hits_remaining…), not (plan, ledger…).
  • Every §3.1 box is unchecked despite ✅ in §0, and §0 still says 199 tests / 4 scenarios.

  • PLAN §2.4:192 says files go through sb.filesystem.write_text/read_text. The code writes with filesystem.write_bytes and does all reads through the exec'd helper. The Sandbox parameters match (workspace.py:568-577).
  • Latency figures: episodes.py:403 and test_lifecycle.py:146 claim reset p50 "1.9 s after". The perf runs runs/20260912T230531Z_perf and 230704Z_perf show 3134 / 3283 ms, which matches the ops doc (3.3 s). Fix the comments. High.
  • Ops doc:35 says the listing is "newest-first", but episodes.py:458 truncates to limit in Dict order before sorting.

3. Boundary / correctness violations

  1. Anyone can kill every live sandbox over HTTP. api.py:175 POST /episodes/sweep?ttl_s=1 needs no auth and CORS allows *; checked, it terminated 2 of 2 live episodes. GET /episodes also publishes every episode id, and the X-Faultline-Episode header is the only MCP auth (PLAN §2.7 turned down tokens on the assumption ids are secret). Enforce a minimum ttl_s, and redact or gate the ops routes. High, low risk.
  2. reap can kill live sandboxes (the "safe reap" box at PLAN:447 is unchecked):
  3. It kills everything by default (modal_app.py:93), and a bare modal run modal_app.py does the same (:181).
  4. With --keep-active, a failed lookup spares nothing (:122-125).
  5. active_sandbox_ids only scans the first 200 records, terminated ones included (episodes.py:542,550).
  6. A sandbox that has been created (episodes.py:184) but not yet saved (:214) isn't spared.
  7. SKILL.md:127 needs updating with any fix. High, medium risk.
  8. The agent's files can reach the hidden-test run.
  9. .faultline_eval is in SKIP_DIRS (workspace.py:37), so anything the agent writes there doesn't show in observe.
  10. grader.py:331 uploads into that directory without clearing it, so an agent-written conftest.py there would load; so would a root conftest.py or pytest.ini.
  11. The directory name is readable in /opt/faultline_helper.py.
  12. Skipped tests aren't counted (grader.py:290).
  13. Fix: clear the directory before upload, add --confcutdir, and assert the expected number of hidden tests. Medium (not seen exploited), low risk.
  14. Unknown execution is recorded as failed.
  15. A WorkspaceError after dispatch (workspace.py:295-303), or a command timeout (mcp_tools.py:241), becomes outcome:error with EINTERNAL.
  16. The grader counts that as a plain failure (grader.py:84), not as maybe-applied.
  17. Timeout detection is string matching (workspace.py:298). Medium-high.
  18. State is persisted after side effects, with an unlocked read-modify-write.
  19. record() runs after the write and after the 3 s ack_lost sleep (mcp_tools.py:206-207).
  20. A retry or resumed worker arriving in that window can fire ack_lost again and lose a ledger row.
  21. If the container dies, the write has landed but no ledger row exists.
  22. Fix: persist a pending row before executing. Medium.
  23. Sleep assumed to set fault timing. worker-crash.json after_ms: 400 assumes the write has landed, but write_file p50 is 1123 ms (ops doc:63). Unverified. Medium.
  24. A sandbox lost during grading becomes a low score, not an ungraded run. grader.py:343 and :119 swallow the WorkspaceError, so the check reads "does not exist". Medium.
  25. Observe on a terminated episode returns the baseline marked "unchanged" (episodes.py:262-271), which hides the agent's edits. Low-medium.

No secret or fault-plan leak into the sandbox found (workspace.py:338,568; episodes.py:131).

  • Assertions that always pass: test_mcp.py:231 and test_episodes.py:120 both end in … or True. Fix or delete. High.
  • Duplicate: test_episodes.py:60 is the same case as test_lifecycle.py:41. Delete one. High.
  • These pin behaviour the docs say is gone: test_mcp.py:240 (sticky fault is None) and test_mcp.py:327 (terminated episode returns EINVAL, not ESANDBOX). Update them together with §2.11.
  • Missing tests: public harness_faults/checks, read_file over the 32,000-char cap, the sweep ttl_s floor, and reap's 200-record spare-set limit. test_scenarios.py:47 passes trivially for worker-crash.
